०मा०1016. – प्रायकर अधिनियम , 1961/( 1961 का 43 ) की धारा 297 के अनुमार भारत सरकार विस मंत्रालय ( राजस्व तथा बीमा विभाग ), नई दिल्ली 
के प्रादेश एफ० सं० 16/ 202/ 67-प्राईटी०बी० दिनांक 25- 3-1969, के अधीन केन्द्रीय सरकार द्वारा प्राधिकृत तथा निर्देशित , निम्नलिखित निर्धारिनियों , जिनके 
वित्तीय वर्ष 1972- 73 के दौरान निर्धारण हुपा है , के नाम और उनसे सम्बन्धित अन्य विवरणों को एतदद्वारा प्रकाशित किया जाता है : 

( 1 ) व्यष्टियों या हि०प्र० कुटुम्यों के रूप में जिनका 1 लाख १० से अधिक प्राय पर निर्धारण हुआ है । 
( 2 ) फर्मों, व्यक्ति-मंगम या कम्पनियों के रूप में , जिनका 10 लाख रु० से अधिक को प्राय पर निर्धारण हुआ है । 

I मभी व्यष्टियों तथा हिन्दू अविभक्त कुटुम्बों के नाम जिनका वित्तीय वर्ष 1972- 73 के दौरान 1 लाख रु० या अधिक की प्राय पर निर्धारण 
हुपा है । 
क्रम सं० निर्धारिति का नाम और पता 

प्रास्थिति 

निर्धारण वर्ष कुल निर्धारित प्राय

मूचित स्थान से / सक रेलवे द्वारा यात्रा करने वाले किसी यात्री पर , ऐसे 
अधिसूचित स्थान से चौबीम किलोमीटर के श्याम के भीतर स्थित किमी

का०मा० 1069.- - नाविक भविष्य निधि योजना 1966 के पैग 37 
सारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए तथा भारत सरकार नौवहन

2604 दिनांक 31- 8- 1973 के माथ पठित अधिसूचना सं० मा० प्रा० 
1430 दिनांक 28- 1-1973 को प्रतिक्रमण करते हुए, नाविक भविष्य 
निधि के न्यामियों के बोर्ड के माथ परामर्श से और अपने साधारण 
प्रशामनिक व्यय पूरे करने के लिये उपलब्ध धन के संसाधनों को दृष्टि 
में रखते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार एतद्वाग 1 अप्रैल , 1974 से उक्त योजना 
के पैग 35 के अन्तर्गत वेय प्रशासनिक प्रभार 14 प्रतिशत निर्धारित 
करती है ।

का०मा० 1071. - रेल यात्रियों पर मीमाकर अधिनियम , 1956 ( 1956 
का 69 ) की धारा 3 की उप -धारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते 
हुए भारत सरकार, रेल मंत्रालय ( रेलवे बोर्ड ) की निनांक 21 मार्च , 
1974 की अधिसूचना संख्या एफ ( एक्म ) I- 74/ 7/ 1-I के साथ पठित 
भारत सरकार , रेल मंत्रालय ( रेलवे बोर्ड ) की अधिसूचना सं० एफ ( एक्स ) 
1 - 73/ 5/ 6-बी का अधिक्रमण करते हुए केन्द्रीय मरकार एतवाग : --- 


S . O . 1069 . - In exercise of the powers conferred by para 
graph 37 of the Seamen s Provident Fund Scheme, 1966 , and 
in supersession of the notification of the Government of Indin 
in the Ministry of Shipping and Transport ( Transport Wing ) 
No . S. O . No. 1430 dated the 28th April, 1973 read with their 
notification No. S. O . 2604, dated the 31st August, 1973, the 
Central Government in consultation with the Board of 
Trustees of the Seamen s Provident Fund and having regard 
to the resources of the Fund available for meeting its normal 
administrative expenses, hereby fixes, with effect from the 
1st April, 1974 the administrative charges payable under para 
graph 3,5 of the said Scheme, at 1- 3 / 4 per cent. 

[ No. 5- MT ( 3 ) / 72 ] 
V . V. SUBRAHMANYAM , Dy. Secy . 


( क ) इम अधिसूचना के साथ संलग्न अनुसूची के कालम II में उल्लिखित 
दरें निश्चित करती हैं । इन्हीं दरों के अनुसार उक्त अनुगूची के कालम I में 
बिनिदिष्ट अधिसूचित स्थान से नक रेलवे द्वारा ले जाये जाने वाले सभी

( ख ) यह निदेश देती है कि उपर्युक्न सीमाकर 1 मई, 1974 
मे उग्राय होगा ।

का०मा० 1076 – चलचित्र अधिनियम , 1952 की धारा 3 की 
उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त अधिकारों का प्रयोग करते हुए , केन्द्रीय 
सरकार ने एतद्वारा निम्नलिखित क्तियों को 1 अप्रैल , 1971 से 
30 जून , 1974 तक , केन्द्रीय फिल्म मेंसर बोर्ड का फिर से सदस्य नियुक्त 
किया है : -- 

1. श्री बी० मार० चोपड़ा 
2. श्रीमती पीना दुग्गल

का० प्रा०1078 -- यत : केन्द्रीय सरकार की राय है कि इसमें उपाबद्ध 
अनुसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट विषयों के बारे में मुम्बई पत्तन न्यास के प्रबन्ध 
तन्त्र से सम्बद्ध नियोजको और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच एक प्रौद्योगिक 
विवाद विद्यमान है ।

निर्देशित करना वांछनीय समझती है ;

( 1947 का 11 ) की धारा 10 की उपधारा (1) के खंर ( घ ) द्वारा

मं० 2, मुम्बई को न्यायनिर्णयन के लिए निर्देशित करती है ।

New Delhi, the 16th April, 1974 
s. o . 1080. - - In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947 ) , the Central Government 
hereby publishes the following award of the Industrial Tribun 
al, Chandigarh in respect of a complaint under Section 33A 
of the said Act filed by Shri Mukhtiar Singh , Pipe Fitter 
which was received by the Central Government on 5th April , 
1974. 

BEFORE SHRI H . R . SODHI, PRESIDING OFFICER , 
INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL , ( CENTRAL ) , CHANDIGARH 

Complaint No. 2 / 49 of 1973 
under Section 33- A of the Industrial Dispute Act, 1947 
Sbri Mukhtiar Singh , Pipe Fitter Token No. 293-AC c / o 
BSL Workers Union Sundernagar 

Complainant. 

Vs . 
1 . Superintending Engineer BSL Admn & Accounts 
Circle, Sundernagar 

Respondent, 
2. Executive Engineer, Field Mechanical Division No . 
II , Sundernagar ( H. P.) 

Respondent . 
Appearances : 

Shri Rattan Lal — for the respondent establishment. 
Shri M. S. Tuggar — for the complainant. 

AWARD 
This complaint arises oul of Reference No. 2 / C of 1971 . 
It was alleged by the complainant that his services were 
terminated by the respondent establishment. Pleadings were 
filed by the parties and issues arising therefrom were framed 
on 3 -11 -1974 . The next date of hearing was fixed on 
11 - 2 - 1974 at Nangal. On that date complainant was not 
present and without his statement the caso could pot proceed 
further . It was stated on behalf of the management that 
the complainant was a proclaimed offender in a case under 
section 307 I. P . C , To give another opportunity to the com 
plainant , the case was adjourned to 19- 3 - 1974 at Chandi 
garh , Again tho complainant was not present. His authorised 
representative, who is president of the B. S. L . Workers Union , 
wanted to make a statement in support of the complaint but 
in the absence of the aggrieved party , the statement of the 
aforesaid representative of the union was without any mean 
ing. The complaint cannot proceed in the absence of the 
complainant. It , therefore, stands dismissed for want of 
prosecution. 
There is no order as to costs. 

H . R. SODI , Industrial Tribunal ( Central ) Chandigarh, 
25- 3- 1974

[PART II 


17 . Section 2A of the I. D . Act, 1947 has been inserted by 
Act 35 of 1965 . This Section 2A is as follows : 
" 2 . A dismissal etc . of an individual workman to bo 

deemned to be an industrial dispute . — Where any 
employer discharges , dismisse , retrenches or other 
wise terminates the services of an individual work 
man , any disputo or difference between that work 
man and his employer conoccted with , or arising 
out of, such discharge , dismissal, retrenchment or 
termination shall be deemed to be an industrial 
dispute notwithstanding that no other workman or 
any union of workmen is a party to the disputo ." 


" In tho instant caso tho omployees whoso services were 

terminated preferred appeals to the Commissioner 
of Labour under S . 39 of the Mysore Shops and 
Establishment Act. After $ .2A of the Industrial 
digputes act, 1947 came into force on 1 - 12 - 1965 
tho Labour Commissioner directed the employees 
to have recourse to tho remedy provided under 
the Industrial Disputes Act. 


18 , In view of the introduction of Section 2A referred to 
above there cannot be any doubt that thự dispute between an 
employer and a single workman becomes an industrial dis 
puto though no other workman or any union of workmen 
Is a party to the dispute . 


19 . Shri Sinha , Labour Officer for the company contends 
that though the present dispute is an industrial dispute within 
the meaning of Section 2 - A of the I. D . Act, 1947, yet the 
reference made to this Tribunal is bad in law bocause Section 
2A is vold and illegal, as It offends the provisions of Article 
14 of tho Constitution . In support of this , ho relics on tho 
ruling of the Calcutta High Court in the case between Juto 
and Juto Goods Buffer Stock Association Vs. The Second 
Jadustrial Tribunal of West Bengal and others , reported in 
1972 , Lab. I. C .503 . This ruling is as follows : 
" Section 2 - A is void since the power of Government 

under Section 10 is un - principled as applied to casco 
of individual dispute deemed to bo industrial dis 
puto under Section 2A . AIR 1937 SC 329 and 
AR 1957 SC 532 Rel. on AIR 1970 Mys 171 and 
AIR 1970 Delhi 60 and AIR 1970 Mad 82 Distin 
guished . AIR 1971 Punj and Har 60 Doubted . 
( para . 30) . 


Tho employees filed potition before the Commissioner 

for Labour withdrawing tho appeals, pursuant to 
which the Commissioner for Labour directed the 
Labour Officer to conciliate the disputo relating to 
guch employees termination . Tho Labour Oncer 
consequently initiated concillation proceedings by 
isguing notices to the employer and the employee. 
In the instant writ petition preferred by the 
employce against such notices issued by the Concilia 
tion Officer , it was contended on his behalf that 
S .24 should be struck down ag being violativo of 
Art 14 of the Constitution of India and its enact 
ment was beyond the powers of the legislature . It 
was further contended that as the disputo relating 
to dischargo, dismissal, retrenchment or termina 
tion having taken place in the instance case prior 
to 1 -12 - 1965 S 2A had no application and tho 
Commissioner of Labour had no jurisdiction to 
direct the Labour Officer to conciliate the dispute 
and the Labour Commissioner should have disposed 
of tho appeals under $ . 39 of the Shops and Estab 
lishments Act . 


The Supremo Court laid down criteria for tho exorciso 

of discretion under Section 10 for collectivo disputes 
do not apply to Section 10 references of individual 
disputes under Section 2 - A because there can be no 
threat of interruption of production or of industrial 
strike or broach of industrial peace in cases of indi 
vidual disputes. 


Negativing the contention that S . 2A was ultra viros of 

the Act and it was violativo of Art. 14 of tho 
Constitution of India , it was held that S .2A could 
pot bo said to bo inconsistent and repugeant to the 
object of tho Act. When A gubsequent Act amendo 
tho carlier Act in guch a way as to incorporate it 
self or as part of itself, into tho carlier Act, then 
the carlier Act must thereafter bo read and cons 
trued as if the altered words had been written into 
the earlier Act with pen and the old words scored 
out so that thereafter there is no need to refer the 
amending Act at all . The words industrial dispute 
occuring in tho preamblo of the Act, are vide 
enough to cover dispute between an employer and 
single employee and as such individual dispute re 
lating to discharge , dismissal or retrenchment could 
not be construed to be outside the scope of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, As the legislaturo was 
competent to introduce S2A under Entry 22 in 
list I to Schedulo 7 of the Constitution of India . 
The said entry is wide enough to include industrial 
and labour disputes between an individual employee 
and his employer . 


Section 10 discretion of the Government is rendered 

unguided on account of its being free to make a 
reference in the caso of one workman and refuso 
a reference in the case of another although both 
of them may be situated in exactly similar circum 
stances (para . 16 ) . 


The Act was enacted for settlement of collectivo disputos 

between employers and workmen . The enactment 
of Seciton 2A destroys the concept of an industrial 
dispute as a collective dispute. The provisions of 
Soction 2 - A do not fit into the general texturo of 
the Act. (para . 18). 


A right to form trade Union Association even by em 

ployers is guaranteed by Article 19 ( 1) (c ) of tho 
Constitution . Any law which is inconsistent ( aş 
Section 2 - A is) with that fundamental right in to 
that extent rendered void by Article 13 . Thus 80 
far as collective disputes are concerned the powers 
under Section 10 are valid ; but those powers aro 
invalid in relation to an industrial dispute deemed 
to be an industrial dispute within Section 2 - A . 
Consequently Section 2 - A which is severable from 
Section 10 is void and as such inoperative in law , 
in spite of Section 10 being valid for collectivo 
disputes being referred to adjudication under Sec 
tion 10 . (Para . 18 )." 


It was further held that tho said S .2A did not offend , 

Art. 14 of the Constitution of India , as thero is an 
intelligiblo differentia which distinguishes an indivi 
dual workman who is discharged, dismissed , ro 
trenched or whose services have beco terminated 
and individual workman who has some other 
grievance in regard to his employment or conditions 
his employment or conditions thereof, and as tho dis 
charge , dismissal or retrenchment or termination of 
service of a workman is of graver consequence to a 
workman than any other griovanco in regard to 
other terms or conditions of employment. 
By enabling the individual workman to raiso 
a disputo in regard to his discharge, dis 
missal or rotrenchment, his causo need not 
bank upon the support of fellow workers as 
it was prior to the coming into forces of S .2A and 
thus tho object for which S .2A was introduced has 
been achieved . So long as discharge, dismissal, 
retrenchment or termination of service is of greater 
degree of grievance consequence to an individual 
workman than any other grievanco in regard to his 
employment or conditions thereof, it is open to the 
Legislature to provide for a special remedy in re 
gard to a workman who is discharged , dismissed , 
retrenched or whoso services aro terminated , while 
such remedy may not be provided for a workman 
whose grievance is of l¢gger gravity . " 


20 . Tho above mentioned ruling supports the contention 
of Shri Sinha that Section 24 of the I. D . Act, 1947 is vold 
and bad in law as it offends the provisions of Article 14 
of tho Constitution . There is however , the ruling of High 
Court of Mygore in the case between P . Janardhana Shetty 
and Union of India and others , reported in 1970 , II, LLJ, 
page 738 , which has taken a contrary ylow . This ruling i 
A9 follows :-- 


SEC , 3 ( ii )] 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 27, 1974 /VAISAKHA 7, 1896 


1115 


29 . In short it will be clear from the above discussions 
that this Tribunal has jurisdiction to entertain this reference 
and the Government was competent to refer this dispute to 
this Tribunal. Hence my findings on point No. I and II are 
as above. 
Point No. 111 


21. In the present case Shri Sinha has not pointed out 
any ruling of the Bombay High Court or of Supremo Court 
in support of his contention that Section 2A is void and 
illegal as it offends the provision of Article 14 of the Cons 
titution . In the absence of specific ruling by the Supreme 
Court or by the Bombay High Court, I rely on the ruling 
of the High Court of Mysore , which has taken a view that 
Section 2 - A is not void . If Section 2 - A is not void and 
illegal the dispute raised by Shri Muthuswami would be 
deemed to be an industrial dispute though it is not espoused 
by other workmen or any union of workmen . Hence the 
Central Government was competent to refor this dispute to 
tbis Tribunal and this Tribunal has got jurisdiction to enter 
tain this reference . 


30 . In connection with the incident, which took place on

22. Shri Sinha for the Company contends that when

" It has been reported that on 10th March , 1971 at

23. Section 2 (s) of the I.D . Act, is as follows : 


" workman means any persons (including an apprentice ) 

employed in any industry to do any skilled or un 
skilled manual supervisory, technical or clerical 
work for hire or reward , whether the terms of 
employment be express or implied and for the pur 
pose of any proceeding under this Act in relation to 
an industrial dispute includes any such person who 
has been dismissed , discharged or retrenched in 
connection with or as a consequence of that dis 
puto or whose dismissal, discharge or retrenchment 
has led to that dispute . . . . . .

25 . The definition of Section 2 (s ) given above clearly 
shows that a workman within the meaning of section 2 ( 3 ) 
includes any person who has been dismissed , discharged or 
retrenched in connection with or as a consequence of that 
dispute or whose dismissal, discharge or retrenchment has led 
to that dispute . Ay Shri Muthuswami hay been dismissed 
by the company, it cannot be said that họ was not a work 
man of the company within the meaning of Section 2 (s ) of 
the I. D . Act, 1947 at the time when ho raised the dispute . 
Hence the contention raised by the company in this respect 
fails .

49 . From the above facts it will be clear that the Standing

44. A reading of the above provisions of the Standing

the mines or the place of work would not fall under clauso

conduct has not taken placo in the department or section 
of the company, 


50 . In the present case , which I am deciding the Standing

orderly or indecent behaviour in the departmont or section 
or any act subversivo of discipline amounts to misconduct. 
It is clear that tho Standing Orders of this company speci 
fically mentions that the drunkenness , or riotous, disorderly 
or indecent behaviour should bo in the department or sec 
tion and not anywhero .

company s employees far away from the work place,

of Shri Muthuswami in the residential colony in question 
far away from the place of work , the domestic crxquiry hield 
against him in respect of the two major charges under

vitiated . There should not have been any enquiry and

swamy under the Standing Ordeig.

tance of about 20 minutes walk . It cannot be thereforo said 
that they aro adjacent to the place of work ,

53 . Shri George Vaz on behalf of the workman relics on 
the ruling reported in 1964, U , LLJ, page 113 in the case 
between Tata Oil Mills Company and its workmen to show 
that the alleged action of Shri Muthuswami is not covered 
by the Standing Orders of the company produced at Ex. 12 / € 
because the quarrel between Shri Muthuswami and Shri Sal 
gaonkur was a private quarrel which took place outside the

59. It appears that the dispute arose on account of quar 
rel over children , that this dispute was purely a privato 
dispute and that it was for Shri Krishna Salgaonkar to take 
action against Shri Muthuswami by filing a criminal com 
plaint. The company should not have interferred with their

54 . The ruling reported in 1964, II, LLJ , page 113 lays 
down as follows : 


60 . It appears from the documentary evidence on record 
that Shri Muthuswami had committed several lapses in the 
past and that he was given warning and cepkure . Even on 
the day of incident in question Shri Muthuswami admits in 
his appeal duted 3 - 9 -71 Ex . 10 / E that he was under the 
influence of alcohol. Taking this fact into consideration and 
the previous records , I am of the view that Shri Muthuswami 
should not be allowed back wages and that he should be 
reinstated as he has put in inore than 21 years of service 
in the company. Hence my finding on point Nos . 4 and 5 
are as above. 


" The relevant Standing Orders inter alla pjovided that 

drunkenness fighting, riotous or disorderly behaviour 
or indecent behaviour within or without the factory 
would be misconduct . A workman was found to 
have assaulted outside the factory another workman 
as the latter was in favour of introduction of in 
centivo bonus scheme, which was opposed by the 
former and his colleagues . The Industrial tribunal 
held that the action of the concerned workinan 
was not covered by the relevant Standing Orders as 
it was a private quarrel between the two workmen 
which took place outside the factory . 


Point No. vi : 

61. For the reasons given above , I pass 
order — 


the following 


ORDER 


In the instant appeal by special leave preferred by the 

employer it was held that if the disorderly or riot 
ous behaviour had some rational connection with 
the employment of the assailant and the victim , such 
act would fall to be covered by the Standing Order . 
It would be unreasonable to include within such 
Standing Order any riotous behaviour outside the 
factory which was the result of purely private and 
individual dispute and in course of which tempers 
of both the contestants became hot. However, in 
the instant case , the assault was not tho result of a 
purely individual or private quarrel between the 
assailant and his victim , but it was referable to 
the difference of opinion between the two in rc 
gard to the introduction of the incentive bonus 
scheme on which the two rival groups of workmien 
in the establishment were sharply divided . Henco 
the conduct of the concerned workman in assault 
ing the other workman surely for the reason that 
the latter was supporting the plcu for more pro 
duction , could not be said to be outside thc purviçw 
of the relevant Standing Order." 


(i) It is hereby declared that the action of the managc 

ment of Sirigao Mines of Messrs Chowgule und 
Company Private Limited , Mormugao Harbour 
(Goa ) in dismissing Shri Muthuswami, Euclid 
Driver , Sirigao Mines, vide their letter dated the 
15th July , 1971 , was not justified and that he is 
cntitled to reinstatement with continuity of service 
but without back wages from tho date of dismissal 

till today i. e. 3 - 3 - 1974 . 
(ii) Award is made accordingly , 
( ii ) No order us to costs. 

N . K . VANI, Presiding Officer

down the same principle as laid down in the rulling reported 
in 1961, I, LLJ, page 546 .

10 . It is our contention that the Standing Orders cannot 
cover the workers at thçir placo of residence even if this 
place of residence is provided as a workers colony for the 
benefit of workery employed in the Company, a worker can 
not be said to be governed by the Standing Orders for 24 
hours of his life all days of the month and year. 


2 . Messrs Chowgulo & Company Pvt. Ltd is a privute 
limited company incorporated in India under the Indian 
Companies Act. The Company is engaged in Iron Ore busi 
ness and is the leading firm in the Iron Ore Industry and is 
number one exporter of Iron ore to Japan and other foreign 
countries . It has iron ore mines at Sirigao , Pale , Costi and 
other areas in Goa and also at Hospet at Mysore State . 
Besides direct extraction of Iron Ore . It is the only Company 
which owiis a Pallestising Plant at Pale and A Ship Building 
Division at Sirigao which are alicd industries connected with 
tho Iron Ore Industry . 


11. If we further study the charges made out against the 
workman and study them in context with the Company 
Standing Orders it will be seen that they apply only to tho 
work place of the workman and not to his place of residence . 
The charges do not specify the exact reference to tho Standing 
Orders. Charge No . 1 will correspond to S . O .No. 19 ( 2 ) (i); 
No. 2 to S . O . No . 19 (2 ) ( 9 ); No. 3 to $ . 0 . No , 19 ( 1) (a ) and 
No. 4 to S . O . 19 ( 1)( e ). Charge Nos. 3 and 4 as per the 
Standing Orders only merit a warning , censuro or find up to 
2 per cent. Charge Nos. 1 and 2 would morit suspension 
for four days at a time, with -holding increment or promo 
tion , or demotion of reduction in grado or removal or dis 
missal from service if he is found guilty of misconduct. It 
is the contention of the Union that those Standing Orders 
cannot bo made applicable to the workman ng all the alle 
gations of misconduct took place far away from the place of 
work . 


3 . Tho Service Conditions of all cmployecs are covered 
by Certified Standing Orders as certified on tho 30th January 
1964 for employecs in the Iron Ore Mines at Sirigao , Costi, 
Palo and Sacorda. This Honourable Tribunal should ask 
the Company to file a Certified Copy of their Standing 
Orders . 


4 . Shri M . Muthuswami, the workman concerned in this 
dispute has put in a continuous service of 21 years with tho 
Company and has a previous service of five ycars as a 
temporary workman . Ho has thus been cagaged by tho 
company for a total servico of 26 years before he was 
terminated by letter dated 12th / 15th July 1971 as referred 
to in the Schedulo to this reference . The original of this 
Jetter will be produced during the hearing. A copy of the 
letter is enclosed as Annexuro I. 


12 . There are caso laws to show that the penalty of dis 
missal is not merited even if there is an assault of ono work 
man on another which is purely of a private naturo and 
which took place outside the factory premises and after the 
working hours. The employer is not justified to tako action 
against the workman . It has been held that when a private 
dispute resulted in an assault by one workman on another 
such assault may attract the relevant provisions of the Penal 
Codo but it cannot attract disciplinary action by tho emplo 
yer under Industrial Law . 


5. A perusal of the 
stated ; 


letter 


of termination conclusion 


" In conclusion , I feel that there are no mitigating cir

compensation in ljeu with effect from the date of

at the work place." 


13 . It is thus not necessary for us to go into tho merits of 
Charges and the enquiry , and find out whether these charges 
have bcen proved by the Enquiry held by the Enquiry Officer 
appointed by the Company. The Company could not frame char 
ges on the basis of the Company Standing Orders neither 
could they hold any enquiry on the basis of the Company 
Standing Orders . Neither was the Company justified in 
taking action on the basis of the Company Standing Orders 
or order the dismissal of the workman . Tho Director wag 
not cmpowered with any such powers under the Standing 
Orders . 

14 . If the allegations and incident as reported to have 
making place had at all taken place on the 10th March , 
1971, the Company would have been justified in Aling a police 
complaint and at the most grant the penalty of ejection from 
the Company quartors and colony which would have suited 
an act of misconduct commited in a workers colony. The 
penalty to be faced by a workman staying in Company quar 
ters for an act of misconduct would bo ejection and removal 
from the Company colony and this would be sufficient 
penalty for the workman for his act of misconduct and vio 
lation of good behaviour, 


6 . It is the contention of the Union that the Order of Dis 
missal is not merited under the Company Standing Orders 
and the Director in issuing this order of dismissal has sur 
passed his powers As granted to him under the Standing 
Orders and therefore this order of dismissal is illegal, null 
and void . 


7. To prove our contention it is necessary to go into the 
wholo backgound of the charges heet and the time, date and 
place of the incident for which the workman was charge 
sheeted and examine whether this incident even if proved 
meritted a dismissal from service. 


15 . There is no misconduct at the workman s place of 
work and no charge of mis-behaviour, drunkenness or or A8 
sault and his work place or at the timo of his duty in the 
mine , and still the Director in the order of dismissal dated 
1.5th July 1971 has stated that he has given his order of 
disinissal because “ This has to be done to maintain strict 
discipline and employer relations at tho work place " . Obvi 
ously the Director has not applied his mind to the nature of 
the charges and the time, place and date of the incident. 
The whole , incident has taken place on the workers day of 
rest and weekly off , it has taken place far away from the 
place of work at the workers colony at Poira village . The 
mines are situated at Sirigao some two miles away. 


8 . The workman was chargeshceted hy letter dated 7th / 
14th April , 1971 for an incident of alleged drunkenness and 
assault that took place on the 10th March , 1971 and which 
took place betwcon two workmen and which was a pro 
jection of the quarrel between the children of the two work 
mon . The incident took place at Poira village which is far 
away from the place of work and the mining cstablishment 
of the Company. The incident also took place when the 
workmen were not on Duty and at the place of residence 
on a Weekly day of rest the 10th March , 1971. It must be 
observed that the Company has built residential barracks 
and quarters at Poira village for the convenience of their 
workmen . That work on are staying at these quarters with 
their wives and families and it will bo far- fetched to say that 
this Poira village is a work place . 

9 . The Standing Orders as Certified were framed in order 
to maintain good discipline of workmen at their work place 
and it would be correct to say that tho workers are goverji 
od by these Standing Orders during their working hours at 
their place of work in the mines and mining establishments. 
Poiru village is outside the mining area and workplace . 


16 . The Dierctor should also have realised that in Goa il

and therefore it was not extra ordinary if the workman 
Muthuswamy had admitted that he had taken a little drink 
and that too on his weckly day of reat when there is no 
work on the nine. On after all is considered as having 
broken the world record on per capita consumption of al 
cohol we do not know whether this observation is correct 
because most of the alcohol manufactured in Goa is smuggl. 
cd outside Goa .

1. Tho Company respectfully submits that the cause of 
Mr. Muthuswami has not beon espoused by any workm¢n 
employed in the Company and has not attained the colour 
of an industrial dispute and, therefore , no Industrial disputo 
exists and the reference ought to be rejected on that ground 
alone. 


Tho workman had thus given a proper way out for the 
Director because even if a Chapter Case was filed against 
tho workman by the Company before a Magistrate . The 
maximum that Magistrate would huve taken would be a 
Boni of Good behaviour or a forfeiture of the Bond. The 
workman was prepared to hand himself over in bondage to 
the Director but the Director turned down this offer and 
bas persisted in his illegal act of dismissal of the workman . 


2 . Without prejudice to what is stated hereinabovo in tho 
cvent of this Hon ble Tribunal coming to the conclusion that 
the reference has been mado under Section 2A of the Jo 
dustrial Disputes Act, 1947 ( hereinafter referred to as “ The 
Act " ) ( it is submitted that there is no case for this Hon blo 
Tribunal arriving at the said conclusion ) , then the referenco 
is bad in law and not maintainablo as it is the submission 
of the Company that Section 24 of the Act is ultra vires the 
Constitution of India . 


19 . Justice demands that the workman Shri M . Muthuswami 
bo reinstated in servico with full back wages as the Com 
pany had no right to deprive the workman and his family 
his bread . The maximum that tho Company could demand 
was a bond of good behaviour and this the workman has 
voluntarily offered . 


20 . Wo therefore pray that the Order of dismissal be set 
aside and that the workman bo allowed to continue his ser 
vico at Sirigao Mines as a Euclid Driver which work he has

compensated for the long period unemployment and star 
vation which has been imposed upon him without legal 
sanction , by being granted full back wages and continuity 
of service from the date of his illegal dismissal - - the 15th 
July , 1971. 


3 . The Company submits that Section 2A of the Act is 
void und illegal as it offends thọ provisions of Article 14 of 
the Constitution in that it confers unbridled , unguided and 
discriminatory powers on the Government under Section 10 
of the Act. The Company submits that the power that tho 
uppropriate Government enjoys under Section 10 results in 
cliscrimination when applied to cases coming within tho 
scope of Section 2A . In the circumstances, the Company 
submits that the Section 2A . is ultar vires and unconstitution 
al and ought to be struck down . It is, therefore , submitted 
that the reference under Section 2A is bad in law .

It was contended on behalf of the workman that the

further admitted that he had not given a copy of the sumo 
to represent him in his disputo with the management. Nor 
believe that Brijlal had not authorised Sri Kanahaiya Singh 
to represent him in his dispute with the management. Nor 
there is any evidence to indicate that at the time when the 
aforesaid settlement was arrived at on behalf of Brijal 
between Sri Kanhaiya Singh and the management it was 

one against the wishes or without the knowledge of Brijlal. 
It appears that it was after the aforesaid seitlement that he

thic Conciliution Officer , Satna had no jurisdiction to deal

muayenent and I am not prepared to disregard it. As 
mcntioned above, the cvidence on the record indicates that

with the management and the Mazdoor Congress entered

Tegard to Motichand and Brijlal after fully safeguarding

had shaken the confidence of the management in both Brijlal 
and Motichand and it could not be said that the management 
bad acted without justification in terminating the services 
of Brijlal and Motichand .

ENTRE 


I now proceed to discuss the oral evidence produced by the 
parties. 

The only witness produced by the management is Shii 
B . K . Singh who was Personnel omcer of the Colliery at 
the relevant time and till December, 1972 . He held the 
Departmental enquiry against the two concerned workmcn . 
According to him the two worknen were present at the 
time of the cnquiry and the witness stated that he took down 
the statements of the witnesses produced by the munagement 
in the presence of the workmen and read over and explained 
the same to the two concerned workmen in Hindi, The wit 
ness further stated that he gave full opportunity to the work 
men to cross - examine the witnesses and the two workmen also 
gave their written statement which were recorded by the wit 
ness who read over and cxplained the same to them in Hindi. 
The workmen did not produce any defence witness . The 
witness was shown the statements of the witnesses recorded 
by him and stated that they were in his own handwriting. 
According to the witness the statements of the witnesses bore 
the signatures of the witnesses and also the left thumb im 
pression and signatures of the workmen except in the case 
of one witness, namely , Shri Nizammudin . The witncss proved 
the statements of the witnesses which were marked Ext. M1- 9 . 
After completing the enquiry Shri B . K . Singh submitted 
his report which was dated 16th October , 1972 and bore 
his signature . The witness proved the report submitted by 
him which was marked Ext. M - 10 . Shri B . K Singh stated 
that he was not biassed against the two workmen and further 
stated that during the enquiry proceedings the two witnesses 
made no complaint against the witness for holding the 
enquiry . 

Cross- examined by Shri T . P . Choudhury on behalf of 
the workmen , the witness stated that he had held other en 
quiries also . Shri B . K . Singh also stated that he had held A 
joint enquiry in respect of the two concerned workmen and 
that he did not give numbers to the witnesses examined by 
bim . Both the workmcn had submitted explanations denying 
the charges levelled against them , Shri B . K . Singh further 
stated that during the enquiry the two workmen admitted 
that they had committed thc alleged misconduct which did 
not strike tho witness as anything unusual. Shri T . P . 
Choudhury drew the attention of Shri B . K . Singh to the 
various pages of Ext. M - 9 and asked him if there were in 
terpolations made in the statoments of the witnessos recorded 
by him during the course of the enquiry . The witness denied 
the suggestion that he had made any interpolations in the 
statements of the witnesses. Shri B . K . Singh further stated 
that he had recorded the statement of the witness , Shri 
B . Prasad in regard to Shri Rumbrich only and that he had 
numbered Shri B . Prasad as PW - 1 . Shri B . K . Singh also 
stated that he had recorded the statement of Shri Gyan Chand 
in respect of the charge -sheets against both the workmen and 
that he had marked Shri Gyan Chand as PW -2 and further that 
he had recorded the statement of Shri Sheo Rash Singh in 
respect of Shri Rambsich alone and that he hud described 
that witness as PW -3 . The witness denied that there wero 
intcrpolations in the 11th and 12th line of page 9 of Ext. M -9 
or that the expression " and when " had been written later 
on . The witness also deniçd the suggestion that in the last 
linc at page 9 of Ext. M - 9 he had written the sentence " I 
have no witness to produce " subscquently even when there was 
no place left for incorporating that sentence. At page 11

peaceful harmonious atmosphere is obvious from the overall 
scheme of these sections . Turning first to S . 33, sub - s. ( 1 ) of 
this section deals with the case of a workman concerned 
in a pending dispute who has been prejudiciully affected by 
an action in regard to a matter cornected with such pending 
dispute and sub - s. (2 ) similarly deals with workman concerned 
in regard to matters unconnccted with such pending disputes. 
Sub -section ( 1 ) bans alteration to the prejudice of the work 
man concerned is the conditions of service applicable to him 
immediately before the commencement of the procecdings 
and discharge or punishment whether by dismissal or other 
wisc of the workman concerned for misconduct connected 
with the dispute without the express permission in writing of 
the authority dealing with the perding proceeding. Sub 
section ( 2 ) places a similar ban in regard to matters not 
connected with the pending dispute but the employer is free 
to discharge or dismiss the workman by paying wages for 
one month provided hc applies to the authority dealing with 
the pending proceeding for approval of the action taken . In 
the case before us we are concerned only with the ban imposed 
against orders of discharge or punishment as contemplated 
by cl. (b ) of the two nub -sections. There are no allegations 
of alteration of the complainant s terms of service. It is 
rot necessary for us to decide whether the present case is 
governed by sub -s . ( 1 ) or sub - s , ( 2 ) because the relevant clause 
in both the sub -sections is couched in similar language and 
we do not find any difference in the essential score and pur 
pose of these two sub -sections a9 far as the controversy bc 
fore us is concerned . It is not worthy that the bar is 
imposed only in regard to action taken for misconduct 
whcther connected or unconnocted with the dispute ." 

Shri S . S . Mukherjce referred to a decision of the Supreme 
Court in Hindustan General Electrical Corporation Limited 
ur.d Bishwanath Prasad and another Supreme Court Labour 
Judgments (8 ) 421 at page 425 in which also the scope of 
applicability of sections 33 and 33A of the Industrial Dis 
putcs Act were considered by the Supreme Court and thọ 
Supreme Court in that case observed as follows :-- - 

" The scope of section 33 and 33A was examined by this 
Court in several cuscs to some of which we shall presently 
refer. Section 33 ( 1 ) has obviously no application to the 
facts of this case . Section 33 ( 2 ) relates to the dismissal, dis 
charge etc . of a workmar. for any misconduct not connected 
with an industrial dispute during the pendency of any conci 
liation proccoding before a Conciliation Officer or a Boarel 
çtc ., unless he had been paid wages for one month and an 
application has been made by the employer to the authority 
before which the proccedirg is pending for approval of the 
action taken by the employer Section 331 cnables a work 
man who has been punished by dismissal or discharge etc ., 
to make a complaint in writing to a Labour Court, Tribunal 
or National Tribunal when an employer contravcnes the pro 
visions of Section 33 during the pendency of proceedings 
before Labour Cout, Tribunal or National Tribunal etc . 
If such it complaint is made, the Labour Court, Tribunal or 
National Tribunal etc . is to adjudicate upon the complaint 
as if it were a disputo referred to or pending before it and 
in accordance with the provisions of the Act submit its 
award to the appropriate Government. In other words , when 
the conditions laid down in section 33A apply a work 
man who is punished as mentioned therein does not have 
to wait for il refercacc of an industrial dispute by an appro 
priate authority under scctior 10 of tho Act for adjudication 
of the dispute but can himself prefer his complaint which 
is to be treated in the same way as a dispute under section 
10 . These sections do not lend themselves to the construction 
that as soon as the Labout Court , Tribunal ctc . finds that 
there has been a violation of Section 33 it should award 
re -instatement. It must go through the procecdirgs which 
would have to be taken under section 10 and it would be 
the duty of Labour Court etc . to cxamine the merits of the 
case in the light of the principles formulated in the Indian 
Iron and Stçel Coi s case ( 1 ) : 


trial Tribunals have been given the power to see whether the 
terminalion of service of a workman is justified acd to give 
appropiratc relief. In cases of dismissal on misconduct, tho 
Tribunal docs not, however , act as a court of Appeal and 
substitute its own judgment for that of the management. It 
will interfere ( 1 ) when there is i want of good faith , ( 2 ) 
when there is victimisation or unfair labour practice, ( 3 ) when 
the management has been guilty of a basic error or viola 
tion of a principle of natural justice , and ( 4 ) when on tho 
materials the finding is completely baseless or perverse ." 

Shri S . S . Mukherjee referred to Ext. M - 5 dated 19th 
November, 1973 which is a letter addressed to the Manager 
of the Colliery concerned and to the Secretary , Bihar Coal 
Miner s Urion , Digwadih by the Deputy Secretary of the 
Ministry of Labour of the Goverament of India in which 
it was stated that in the opinion of the Central Govern 
ment the clispute raised by the two workmen was not " fit 
for reference to an Industrial Tribunal for adjudication be 
cause the purishment was imposed on the basis of findings 
of proper domestic enquiry and the management has not 
acted mala fide or practised victimisation " . 

In my view Ext. M - 5 , that is , the view of the ministry 
of Labour, contained in that letter was only an opioion ex 
pressed by thc Deputy Secretary of the Central Government 
and that opirion is not binding on this Tribunal. 

In view of the fact that the provisions of section 33 ( 2 )(b ) 
of the Industrial Disputes Act are of a mandatory mature 
and in view of the concession mude by Shri S . S . Mukherjee 
that no application for approval of the action taken by the 
management against the two workmen wus sought for from 
this Tribunal, it must be held that the managemcot clearly 
violated the provisions of section 33 (2 )(b ) of the Act but I 
agrco with Shri S . S . Mukherjee that the violation of the 
provisions of section 33 ( 2 )( b ) did not in itself entitled the 
two workmen to be re - instated at thcir services . It only 
entitled the workmen to file a complaint before this Tribunal 
under section 33A of the Industrial Disputes Act. I also 
agrce with Shri T . P . Choudhury that if an application for 
approval of the action of the dismissal taken by the managc 
ment against the two workmen had been filed by the man 
agement under section 33 ( 2 )( b ) the two concerned workmen 
in his complaint would have got an opportunity to show that 
their dismissal was un -warranted . In view of thọ mandatory 
provisions of section 33 2 )( b ) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 
it was the duty of the management to have filed an appli 
cation under that section for approval of their action in dis 
missing the two workmen and in not doing so they clearly 
violated the provisions of that section . 

The rext contention of Shri T . P . Choudhury is that the 
Departinentul enquiry hell against thc two workmen by 
Shri B . K . Singh way a shame enquiry and was not in accord 
ance with the principles of natural justice and hence the 
Orders of dismissal passed against the two workmen aro 
vitiated and the workmen , are therefore cntitled to be re 
instated in their services, Shri T . P . Choudhury further 
submitted that during tho enquiry proceedings no opportunity 
was given to the two workmen to defend themselves and the 
enquiry officer was guilty of making many interpolations 
in the various statements produced on bchalf of the manage 
ment. Shri T . P . Choudhury further submitted that the 
erquiry proccedings should have been recorded in Hindi as 
the two workmen do not hnow English and an opportunity 
should have been given to the workmen to be assisted by an 
English knowing co -worker . According to Shri T . P . Chou 
Thury the management was determined to dismiss the two 
workmen because they were persora -non - grata with the man 
agement and therefore, the cnquiry officer made several inter 
polations in the statements of the witnesses produced by the 
management and violated the principles of natural justice . 

As regards the taking down of the proccedings during the 
cnquiry ir English , the law has heen laid down by the Madras 
High Court in Management of "Woodbariar" and workers 
in " Woodbariar " , 1961 Vol. 2 Indian Factories and Labour 
Reports page 358, in which it was held as follows: " Where 
the dismissal of an employee is mala fide, or there was 
unfair labour practice , or victimisation , or the findings were 
perverse or baseless or the management had been guilly of 
any basic error or violatior of any principles of natural 
justice , the Labour Tribunal has jurisdiction to interfore . 
But where the dismissal is as a result of bona fide cnquiry the 
charges made not being actuated by any ulterior motive , 
it has no jurisdiction to interfere " . 


" The law laid down by the Supreme Court in case of dis 
missal of a workman on nuisconduct has beers dealt with 
by that Hon ble Court in Indian Tron and Steel Company 
Limited and another Versiis their workmen , 1958 Supreme 
Court Reports page 667 at page 685 and the relevant portion 
of the Supreme Court s observations in thal case are thus - 

" Undoubtedly , the macagement of concern has power to 
direct its own internal adininistration and discipline ; but 
the power is not unlimited and when a dispute arises Indus
